Tool result:
Run systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don't fix issues — document them for other commands to address.

This is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what's measurable and verifiable in the implementation.

## Diagnostic Scan

Run comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.

### 1. Accessibility (A11y)

**Check for**:
- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)
- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps
- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions
- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators

**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)

### 2. Performance

**Check for**:
- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change
- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization

**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)

### 3. Theming

**Check for**:
- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change

**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)

### 4. Responsive Design

**Check for**:
- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants

**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)

### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)

Check against ALL the **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).

**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)

### Anti-Patterns Verdict

### Detailed Findings by Severity

Tag every issue with **P0-P3 severity**:
- **P0 Blocking**: Prevents task completion — fix immediately
- **P1 Major**: Significant difficulty or WCAG AA violation — fix before release
- **P2 Minor**: Annoyance, workaround exists — fix in next pass
- **P3 Polish**: Nice-to-fix, no real user impact — fix if time permits

For each issue, document:

- **Location**: Component, file, line
- **Category**: Accessibility / Performance / Theming / Responsive / Anti-Pattern
- **Impact**: How it affects users

- **Suggested command**: Which command to use (prefer: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset)

### Patterns & Systemic Issues

Identify recurring problems that indicate systemic gaps rather than one-off mistakes:
- "Hard-coded colors appear in 15+ components, should use design tokens"
- "Touch targets consistently too small (<44px) throughout mobile experience"

### Positive Findings

Note what's working well — good practices to maintain and replicate.

## Recommended Actions

List recommended commands in priority order (P0 first, then P1, then P2):

Map findings to the most appropriate command. End with `$impeccable polish` as the final step if any fixes were recommended.

After presenting the summary, tell the user:

**IMPORTANT**: Be thorough but actionable. Too many P3 issues creates noise. Focus on what actually matters.

**NEVER**:

- Provide generic recommendations (be specific and actionable)
- Skip positive findings (celebrate what works)
- Forget to prioritize (everything can't be P0)
- Report false positives without verification

Remember: You're a technical quality auditor. Document systematically, prioritize ruthlessly, cite specific code locations, and provide clear paths to improvement.

64:## Shared design laws
65-
66-Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.
67-
68-### Color
69-
70-- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
71-- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
72-- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
73-  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
74-  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
75-  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
76-  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
77-- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.
78-
79-### Theme
80-
81-Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."
82-
83-Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.
84-
85-"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.
86-
87-### Typography
88-
89-- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
90-- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.
91-
92-### Layout
93-
94-- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
95-- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
96-- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.
97-
98-### Motion
99-
100-- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
101-- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.
102-
103-### Absolute bans
104-
105-Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.
106-
107-- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
108-- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
109-- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
110-- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
111-- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
112-- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.
113-

117-- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

97:## Accessibility & Inclusion
98-
99-**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.
100-
101-- Text contrast is at least 4.5:1 against its actual rendered background, and at least 3:1 for large text and for the visual boundaries of interactive controls. Students read dense argument text on this platform for hours at a time.
102-- Every interactive element has a visible focus indicator.
103-- `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected. Motion is never load-bearing for understanding or for completing a task.
104-- Interactive targets meet the 44x44px minimum, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text.
105-- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
106-- Contrast is verified against what actually renders in the browser, not against the token values a theme is assumed to apply. The theme layer has shipped a light card on a near-black page while the token file read as correct.
====DESIGN====
147-What they do share is restraint about meaning. In both, colour is a signal. Green, red and amber say something happened. Nothing is coloured to be warm, to look premium, or to fill a space. Depth comes from hairlines and background tone rather than shadow, and hierarchy comes from size, weight and position rather than hue.
148-
149:Inside the Workroom, individual rooms may commit fully to their own world. The eight Activity Centre worlds, the scoped bootcamps and the inbox each carry a painted identity behind a wrapper class. That is sanctioned and it is what an academy is: neutral corridors, characterful rooms. The corridor never takes on a room's paint.
150-
151-**Key Characteristics:**
152-- Two registers, one institution: a dark marble Hall at the door, a light neutral Workroom inside.
153-- Colour means something or it is absent.
154-- Flat surfaces, hairline separation, no drop shadows.
155-- Precise and quiet controls that stay out of the reader's way.
156-- Rooms may be worlds; corridors never are.
157-
158-## Colors
159-
160-Two grounds, one shared vocabulary of meaning.
161-
162-### Primary
163-
164-- **Workroom Ink** (`#121212`): the Workroom's near-black. It is the text colour, the fill of every primary button, and the colour of the focus ring. The Workroom has no accent, so ink carries emphasis by itself.
165-- **Hall Bronze** (`#c6a467`): the Hall's warm light. Hairline borders on the call to action, a sheen across it on hover, and the selection highlight. It exists only on the Hall's night ground and never crosses into the Workroom.
166-
167-### Secondary
168-
169-- **Hall Cyan** (`#8ad8e2`): the Hall's cool instrument note against the bronze, and the colour of its focus outlines. Rare by design; it marks precision where bronze marks warmth.
170-- **Teacher Amber** (`#c7841f`): the single accent of the teacher workspace, with a hue-locked ramp for hover (`#af7318`), text on tinted fills (`#895910`) and a light step (`#e0a648`). Deliberately theme-independent: the workspace is always the light format. It is the one accent in the product, and it belongs to the operator, not the student.
171-
172-### Tertiary
173-
174-Individual rooms carry their own palettes behind a wrapper class and are documented in their own surface briefs, not here. The wrappers in use are `.classroom-root`, `.mc-bootcamp`, `.ff-bootcamp`, `.rc-bootcamp`, `.topic-abstract`, `.cs-punk`, and the eight Activity Centre worlds.
175-
176:`.classroom-root` was added to this list on 2026-09-08, after an audit found the classroom running on Outfit and a warm palette while this record still described it as neutral Workroom chrome. It is a sanctioned world, not drift: the warmth and the typeface are deliberate and they are what makes the classroom feel like a study rather than a dashboard. The record was wrong, not the code. Its brief is `.impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md`. Note that this is the one sanctioned world that is also a corridor, so the rule below about corridors never being worlds now has exactly one documented exception.
177-
178-### Neutral
179-
180-- **Workroom Page** (`#fcfcfc`) and **Card** (`#ffffff`): the page ground and the surface that sits on it. One step of separation, no more.
181-- **Workroom Raised** (`#f7f7f7`) and **Fill** (`#f2f2f2`): the two tonal steps available for layering and for resting fills.
182-- **Workroom Ink Muted** (`#737373`): secondary text. Measures 4.62:1 on the page, which clears the floor with almost nothing to spare. Do not lighten it.
183-- **Workroom Hairline** (`#e0e0e0`) and **Field** (`#e6e6e6`): the borders that do the work shadows used to.
184-- **Hall Marble** (`#eae5da`), with secondary (`#a6a093`) and muted (`#6e695e`) steps: warm off-white text on the night ground.
185-
186-### State (semantic only, never decorative)
187-
188-- **Correct** (`#10b77f`) and **Incorrect** (`#f43e5c`): a student's attempt was right or wrong. Held under their own names, separate from system status, so a future shift to one cannot silently reframe the other.
189-- **Warn** (`#f59f0a`): a bonus, a stretch, a non-blocking notice.
190-- **Success** (`#21c45d`), **Warning** (`#f59f0a`), **Destructive** (`#dc2828`): system status, not student performance.
191-
192-### Named Rules
193-
194-**The Ground Decides Rule.** A warm colour is legible on the Hall's night and illegible on the Workroom's page. Never carry a colour across the seam on the strength of liking it; measure it against the ground it will land on first.
195-
196-**The Meaning-Only Rule.** A colour appears in the Workroom because something is right, wrong, cautionary, or dangerous. There is no fifth reason. If a surface feels flat and the impulse is to add a colour, the answer is type and space.
197-
198-**The Named Ground Rule.** No colour is written as a literal hex in a component. It resolves from a token, and a token that resolves to nothing is a defect, not a subtle effect. Three colour names in this codebase pointed at variables that were never defined, and the product silently lost its focus ring for five months.
199-
200-## Typography
201-
202-**Workroom Body:** Lexend (with Outfit, then the system stack)
203-**Hall Display:** Fraunces (with Iowan Old Style, Times New Roman, serif)
204-**Hall Inscription:** Cinzel (with Trajan Pro, serif)
205-**Mono:** IBM Plex Mono
206-**Question Lock:** Outfit, held deliberately for LSAT question surfaces
207-
208-**Character:** The Workroom is one humanist sans doing everything, sized and weighted rather than swapped. The Hall is a serif institution: an optical serif for its headings and an inscriptional face for its few carved lines. The split is the whole typographic idea. Inside, type gets out of the way; at the door, type is the architecture.
209-
210-### Hierarchy
211-
212-- **Title** (600, 24px, line-height 1, tracking -0.025em): card and section headings in the Workroom.
213-- **Body** (400, 14px rising to 16px at 640px and above, line-height 1.6): everything a student reads in the Workroom chrome.
214-- **Question** (400, 16px, line-height 1.6, Outfit): LSAT stimulus and answer choices. Font-locked to Outfit even though the rest of the app moved to Lexend, so question text does not shift when the shell's face changes.
215-- **Label** (500, 14px): button and control labels.
216-- **Hall Display** (380, fluid, tracking -0.015em, Fraunces): Hall headings. The unusually light 380 weight is the point; it reads as carved rather than printed.

## user

Tool result:
- **Hall Display** (380, fluid, tracking -0.015em, Fraunces): Hall headings. The unusually light 380 weight is the point; it reads as carved rather than printed.
- **Hall Inscription** (500, tracking 0.05em, Cinzel): the Hall's few short carved lines. Never body copy.
- **Hall Mono** (400, 13px, tracking 0.04em, IBM Plex Mono): the Hall's instrument labels.

### Named Rules

**The One Face Rule.** The Workroom uses a single family. Hierarchy is built from size, weight and space. A second family inside the Workroom is a defect unless it is the Outfit question lock.

**The Question Never Moves Rule.** LSAT question text is font-locked. A change to the shell's typeface must not reflow a passage a student is mid-way through reading.

**The Serif Stays At The Door Rule.** Fraunces and Cinzel belong to the Hall. A serif appearing in the Workroom chrome means the seam has leaked.

## Layout

The Workroom is a centred single column with generous side gutters. The container centres by default, carries 32px of horizontal padding, and stops widening at 1400px.

Breakpoints are the framework defaults up to 1280px, with the largest step pulled in to 1400px. Body type steps from 14px to 16px at 640px, and form controls are forced to 16px below 768px so that iOS does not zoom the page when a field takes focus. Named breakpoints `phone` (up to 600px), `tablet` (601 to 900px) and `desktop` (901px and up) exist for bootcamp surfaces.

Spacing runs on a 4px base. The steps in real use are 4, 8, 16, 24 and 32. Card padding is 24px on all sides; button padding is 8px vertical and 16px horizontal.

Reading measure matters more here than in most products. Passage and stimulus text is held to a comfortable line length rather than allowed to run the full container width.

### Named Rules

**The Reading Measure Rule.** Any surface carrying an LSAT passage, stimulus, or explanation constrains its own line length. The container's maximum is a ceiling for layout, never a licence for prose.

## Elevation & Depth

**Flat, with hairline separation.** Surfaces sit on the page rather than above it. A card is distinguished from its ground by a one-pixel border and, where more is needed, by one step of background tone. There are no drop shadows in the Workroom.

This is a decision, not a description. The codebase currently ships five shadow tokens tuned for a dark ground, from `0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` up to `0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.7)`, and applies the smallest of them to every card. Because the app ships light by default, those shadows land at 40 percent black under a white card. The light theme never redefined them, and the one glow token is white, so it is invisible on white. The tokens are retired by this record and the code has not caught up yet.

The Hall is the exception and keeps its own depth: fixed ambience gradients, a grain overlay, and an inset highlight on its call to action. That depth is atmospheric rather than structural, and it does not transfer.

### Named Rules

**The Flat Rule.** Depth in the Workroom comes from a hairline or a tonal step. A `box-shadow` on a Workroom surface is a defect.

**The Two-Step Rule.** Page, then card, then at most one raised step. A surface that needs a fourth level of layering is a surface that needs restructuring.

## Shapes

Corners are gently rounded and consistent: 8px for cards and containers, 6px for buttons and inputs, 4px for the smallest chips and insets, and a full pill only for genuine badges. The scale derives from one 0.5rem radius token, so a change to the base moves the whole system together.

The Hall uses a near-square 2px radius instead. Its edges are cut, not softened, which is what separates a carved surface from a card.

Borders are one pixel. There is no second border weight in the Workroom.

### Named Rules

**The Hairline Rule.** One pixel, always. A thicker border used as a coloured accent stripe on the leading edge of a surface is banned, including when faked with an inset shadow. Active state is carried by background tone.

## Components

### Buttons

- **Character:** precise and quiet. A button is a plain rectangle with a label. It darkens slightly on hover and does nothing else. It does not lift, does not cast a shadow, and does not scale on press.
- **Shape:** gently rounded (6px).
- **Primary:** ink fill (`#121212`), page-coloured label, 8px by 16px padding.
- **Outline:** page background, one-pixel field border, ink label.
- **Ghost:** no fill at rest, a tonal fill on hover.
- **Sizes:** default 44px tall, small 40px, large 48px, icon 44 by 44. Every size clears the 44px touch minimum by being that height, never by relying on wrapped text or padding accidents.
- **Focus:** a solid two-pixel ink outline, offset two pixels from the edge. This is the only focus treatment in the Workroom.
- **Hall CTA:** 60px tall, near-square corners, transparent fill over a bronze hairline, with a bronze sheen that sweeps across on hover and a cyan outline on focus.

### Cards / Containers

- **Corner:** 8px.
- **Background:** card white on the page ground.
- **Border:** one-pixel hairline. This is what separates the card, not a shadow.
- **Shadow:** none.
- **Padding:** 24px, with header, content and footer sharing that inset.

### Inputs / Fields

- **Style:** page background, one-pixel field border, 6px corners, 44px minimum height, 8px by 12px padding.
- **Focus:** the same two-pixel ink outline as buttons, offset two pixels. Do not express focus as a border colour change alone; a border shift is too quiet to serve as the only indicator.
- **Mobile:** 16px text below 768px, so focusing a field never zooms the viewport.

### Navigation

Neutral chrome throughout. The sidebar runs one tonal step off the page ground with a hairline edge, and the active item is marked by background tone rather than by a coloured rule on its leading edge.

### Signature Component: the scoped world

The product's distinctive pattern is not a control, it is a container. A room commits fully to its own palette, typefaces and atmosphere behind a single wrapper class, and nothing inside it leaks out. Scene layers are `aria-hidden` furniture painted beneath the content, so the accessible tree is identical whether the room is painted or bare. A new world is added by writing a new wrapper and its prefixed tokens, never by adding hex to a shared component.

## Do's and Don'ts

### Do:

- **Do** resolve every colour from a token, and treat a token that resolves to nothing as a defect. Verify a new token renders before documenting it.
- **Do** give every interactive element a visible focus indicator: a solid two-pixel ink outline offset two pixels in the Workroom, cyan in the Hall.
- **Do** check contrast against the ground the colour will actually land on, in the theme that actually ships, in a browser. The theme file has read as correct while the page rendered a light card on a near-black ground.
- **Do** separate surfaces with a one-pixel hairline and one tonal step.
- **Do** keep colour semantic: correct, incorrect, warning, danger. Nothing else earns it.
- **Do** scope a new room's identity behind its own wrapper class with prefixed tokens, the way the bootcamps and the eight Activity worlds already do.
- **Do** hold LSAT passage and stimulus text to a readable line length, and leave it on the Outfit question lock.
- **Do** end every world's stylesheet with a `prefers-reduced-motion` block that stops continuous motion rather than speeding it up.

### Don't:

- **Don't** add a `box-shadow` to a Workroom surface. Depth is hairline and tone.
- **Don't** carry a Hall colour into the Workroom. Bronze reads 8.58:1 on the Hall's night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom's page.
- **Don't** introduce a warm or metallic accent into the Workroom to make it feel premium. Premium here is depth, hierarchy and type.
- **Don't** remove the browser's focus outline without shipping a replacement in the same rule. `outline-none` with no working ring is worse than leaving the default alone.
- **Don't** animate the focus indicator. A `transition-all` on a control means the ring fades in, which delays the one signal a keyboard user depends on.
- **Don't** use a border thicker than one pixel as a coloured accent stripe, including when faked with an inset shadow.
- **Don't** put a second typeface into the Workroom chrome, and don't let a serif cross in from the Hall.
- **Don't** animate layout properties. Animate `opacity`, `transform` and `color`. No bounce, no elastic, no lift on hover.
- **Don't** write a hex value into a component. If a room needs its own colour, promote it to named tokens behind that room's wrapper.

# Read, Recall, Colour section briefing page: design critique

Date: 2026-09-08. Method: `$impeccable critique` on
`src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx`, two isolated
passes (a design review with live desktop inspection, and the deterministic
detector) woven into one report. Related: [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]], [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]].

## Design health score

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 2 | "One take, no pause" and "about 25 minutes" are not on the page |
| 2 | Match system / real world | 3 | "The Recall." reads as a typo beside three imperatives |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 3 | Back is 44x44 and works; nothing else needed |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2 | Three micro-label styles; the two H2s render at different weights |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | No warning that the browser will ask for the microphone |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | A " +" text glyph is the only disclosure affordance |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 2 | Space is the runner's only key but does nothing here |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | 3 | Chip and H1 both say the product name; foot restates the notice |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | Mic-denied case has no hint on this page |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 4 | The collapsed long version is exactly right |
| **Total** | | **27/40** | **Acceptable, upper end** |

## Anti-patterns verdict

Does it look AI-made? No. Single column, four numbered steps, one primary
action, Fraunces title on cream, no hero metric, no icon grid, no gradient text,
no side stripes, no em dashes in the rendered DOM. The one drift toward generic
is the Start button: a landing-page pill with a hover lift and drop shadow,
written for the dark hero and inherited by this cream task page.

Deterministic scan: `npx impeccable --json` on the page returned zero findings.
The directory scan of `components/` returned 53 advisory findings, none touching
this page or `section-launch.css` (47 are in `activity-scenes.css`). No false
positives to triage. Browser overlay could not run: impeccable 4.0.4 ships no
`detect.js`, and the page has no CSP, so this is a tooling gap and not a page
problem. Cognitive load: 1 of 8 checks failed (working memory), which is low.

## Overall impression

The structure is right and the copy is the best on the platform. The misses are
content gaps and vocabulary drift, not layout. One of them matters: the page was
edited on 2026-09-07 on the belief that the one-take fact survived in Step 4.
It did not. It lives in `instructions.rules[0]`, which this page never renders.

## What's working

- A page, not a modal, with all four steps open and numbered. "Laughing is
  fine." and "It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours." earn their place.
- The hero classes are honestly re-grounded for paper, and the comments say why.
- Accessibility basics hold, measured live: H1 H2 H2 H3, Back 44x44, 2px focus
  rings, no live-region noise.

## Priority issues

**[P1] The two facts she most needs before Start are not on the page.**
Verified live with the long version open: zero hits for "one take"; "minute"
only inside the collapsed essay's sixth heading. The code comment at
`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:63` claims Step 4 carries it. Step 4 does not.
Fix: one plain sentence beside the foot Start: "It runs in one take, about
twenty-five minutes, and it does not pause." Or render `briefing.closer`,
which the type describes as "the last beat before the Start action" and which
nothing renders. Correct the comment. Command: `$impeccable clarify`.

**[P1] The calming notice is styled as a fifth step card.**
`.sl-notice` and `.sl-bp-list li` share fill, hairline, and radius. The one
sentence written to lower her shoulders reads as procedure.
Fix: take the box off it. A plain ink line under the CTA. Command: `$impeccable layout`.

**[P2] Step body lines run about 105 characters.** The essay caps at 68ch; the
step list has no measure. Fix: `max-width: 62ch` on `.sl-bp-list li p` and
`.sl-notice`. Command: `$impeccable typeset`.

**[P2] Three micro-label styles on one page, and mismatched H2s.** Mono chip,
Inter-uppercase h2, Inter-uppercase step number, while `.sl-label` is declared
the one style and goes unused. The summary's h2 inherits body weight, so the
only help on the page looks like a stray sentence. Fix: put step numbers and
h2s on `.sl-label`; give the summary the same h2 treatment and a real chevron.
Command: `$impeccable typeset`.

**[P2] The lede is silently overridden.** `.sl-essay p` outranks `.rd-lede`, so
the 20px italic lede renders at 16.5px. Fix: `.sl-essay p.rd-lede`.
Command: `$impeccable polish`.

**[P3] Landing-page button on a task page.** Drop the transform and outer
shadow on `.sl-briefing-page .sl-start`; keep the fill darkening.
Command: `$impeccable quieter`.

## Persona red flags

- **Jordan (first-timer):** reads "Press space" in Step 4 and looks for a Space
  button; presses Start and meets a microphone permission dialog with no warning.
- **Sam (screen reader):** an `h2` nested inside `summary` is exposed
  inconsistently; some readers drop it from the headings list, the very outline
  the h2 was added to fix.
- **Casey (mobile):** the reassurance sits at the top and the Start she presses
  is 1,000px below it. Same fix as the first P1. Mobile was reasoned from the
  CSS, not seen: the viewport resize did not take.
- **A coaching client at 9pm:** wants "how long is this and can I stop if my
  roommate walks in." The page answers neither, and nothing on it is addressed
  from her coach.

## Minor observations

- Chip and H1 both say "Read, Recall, Colour"; the chip is redundant here.
- The head's side-by-side layout never fits at 840px, so the CTA always wraps
  under the tagline. What ships is accidental.
- Empty tagline still renders an empty `p` with top margin.
- Foot copy repeats "begins" and restates the notice's fact.
- Summary focus ring is `--rd-ink-2`, buttons are `--rd-ink`.
- `.sl-bp-meta` is defined and unused.

## Questions to consider

1. Why is the one sentence written to calm her styled identically to the four things she has to do?
2. If the runner's only key is Space, what would happen if Space started the section from here?
3. What would be missing if the page were only the notice, the four steps, and one Start at the foot?

## Recommended order

1. `$impeccable clarify`: the one-take and 25-minute sentence at the foot, render `closer`, fix the comment.
2. `$impeccable layout`: un-box the notice.
3. `$impeccable typeset`: 62ch measure, one label style, matching H2s, lede fix.
4. `$impeccable quieter`: the Start button's lift and shadow.
5. `$impeccable polish` last.

## assistant

Now the components. I'll read the frame and both doors in one pass, plus the preview harness.

    49	const EASE = [0.22, 1, 0.36, 1] as const;
    50	
    51	/** One authored entrance, shared by every block below the hero. */
    52	const reveal = {
    53	  initial: { opacity: 0, y: 28 },

    55	  viewport: { once: true, margin: "-12% 0px" },

    57	} as const;
    58	
    59	const STEP_KINDS = ["read", "tell", "colour", "recall"] as const;
    60	
    61	/**
    62	 * The little painted scene above each step.

    95	          <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "80%" }} />
    96	          <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "64%" }} />
    97	        </div>
    98	      </div>

   127	 *
   128	 * There are two doors and one design. The library is where she picks tonight's

    13	/** How many past runs the history block shows. */
    14	const RUNS_SHOWN = 3;
    15	
    16	export interface SectionLaunchProps {
    17	  title: string;
    18	  subtitle: string | null;
    19	  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
    20	  /** Stimuli in this section, from the assignment snapshot. */
    21	  itemCount?: number;
    22	  /** The clock this section is run against. */
    23	  timeGoalMinutes?: number | null;
    24	  /** `PT105.S2`. Scopes the history read to this section. */
    25	  sourceSection?: string | null;
    26	  /**
    27	   * Whose runs to read. Passed to the QUERY, not used as a display filter: the
    28	   * admin SELECT policy on `activity_section_grades` returns every student's
    29	   * rows, and no flag on this component can undo that. Only the `.eq` can.
    30	   */
    31	  studentId?: string | null;
    32	  /**
    33	   * Whether to show run history at all. False for a teacher reading a student's
    34	   * page and for the authoring preview, where "her last three runs" is either
    35	   * somebody else's record or nobody's.
    36	   */
    37	  showHistory?: boolean;
    38	  onStart: () => void;
    39	  onExit?: () => void;
    40	}
    41	
    42	type HistoryState =
    43	  | { kind: "idle" }
    44	  | { kind: "loading" }
    45	  | { kind: "ready"; runs: RecentSectionRun[] }
    46	  | { kind: "failed" };
    47	
    48	/**
    49	 * The door to a Read, Recall, Colour section.
    50	 *
    51	 * What it has to do that the editorial intro could not: fit. The page it
    52	 * replaces ran 2778px, put its one button below the fold and grew a sticky dock
    53	 * to compensate. This is one card, Start in the top right, and the long briefing
    54	 * folded into a drawer that opens a beat at a time.
    55	 *
    56	 * Everything numeric on it is derived from runs she has already done -- see
    57	 * `recall/runStats.ts`, which holds the arithmetic and the refusals. Nothing
    58	 * here is scored, predicted, or filled in when the evidence is missing.
    59	 */
    60	export function SectionLaunch({
    61	  title,
    62	  subtitle,
    63	  instructions,
    64	  itemCount,
    65	  timeGoalMinutes,
    66	  sourceSection,
    67	  studentId,
    68	  showHistory = false,
    69	  onStart,
    70	  onExit,
    71	}: SectionLaunchProps) {
    72	  const [history, setHistory] = useState<HistoryState>({ kind: "idle" });
    73	  const noKeyboard = useNoKeyboard();
    74	
    75	  const canRead = showHistory && Boolean(sourceSection) && Boolean(studentId);
    76	
    77	  useEffect(() => {
    78	    if (!canRead) {
    79	      setHistory({ kind: "idle" });
    80	      return;
    81	    }
    82	    // A resolution that arrives after this effect has been superseded, or after
    83	    // the component is gone, must not land. Without the guard a slow first read
    84	    // can overwrite a fast second one and show the wrong section's runs.
    85	    let live = true;
    86	    setHistory({ kind: "loading" });
    87	    readMyRecentSectionGrades(RUNS_SHOWN, {
    88	      sourceSection: sourceSection ?? undefined,
    89	      studentId: studentId ?? undefined,
    90	    })
    91	      .then((result) => {
    92	        if (!live) return;
    93	        setHistory(result.failed ? { kind: "failed" } : { kind: "ready", runs: result.runs });
    94	      })
    95	      .catch(() => {
    96	        if (live) setHistory({ kind: "failed" });
    97	      });
    98	    return () => {
    99	      live = false;
   100	    };
   101	  }, [canRead, sourceSection, studentId]);
   102	
   103	  // Derived inside the memo rather than above it. A `[]` literal computed on
   104	  // every render is a new array every render, so hoisting it would give the
   105	  // memo a dependency that never compares equal and recompute the stats each
   106	  // pass -- a memo that memoises nothing.
   107	  const runs = useMemo(
   108	    () => (history.kind === "ready" ? history.runs : []),
   109	    [history],
   110	  );
   111	  const stats = useMemo(
   112	    () => statsFor(runs, timeGoalMinutes ?? null),
   113	    [runs, timeGoalMinutes],
   114	  );
   115	
   116	  return (
   117	    <SectionLaunchFrame
   118	      title={title}
   119	      subtitle={subtitle}
   120	      instructions={instructions}
   121	      onExit={onExit}
   122	      meta={
   123	        <>
   124	          {sourceSection ? `${sourceSection} · ` : ""}
   125	          {typeof itemCount === "number" ? `${itemCount} stimuli` : ""}
   126	          {timeGoalMinutes ? ` · est ${timeGoalMinutes} min` : ""}
   127	        </>
   128	      }
   129	      primaryAction={
   130	        <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
   131	          Start the section
   132	          <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
   133	        </button>
   134	      }
   135	      closingAction={
   136	        <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
   137	          Start now
   138	          <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
   139	        </button>
   140	      }
   141	      /* Warned, not refused: Joshua's call, 2026-09-04. She is told what the
   142	         run needs and left free to make the call herself, which is why nothing
   143	         here disables Start. The run itself carries an on-screen way to advance
   144	         so that going ahead is a real choice rather than a trap. */
   145	      notice={
   146	        noKeyboard ? (
   147	          <p className="sl-notice" role="status">
   148	            This one is built for a laptop. It records you for the whole section
   149	            in one take, so a call or a locked screen ends the run, and the
   150	            microphone on a phone hears you less well. You can carry on if you
   151	            want to.
   152	          </p>
   153	        ) : null
   154	      }
   155	      strip={showHistory ? <StatStrip state={history} stats={stats} /> : null}
   156	    >
   157	      {showHistory && <RunHistory state={history} />}
   158	    </SectionLaunchFrame>
   159	  );
   160	}
   161	
   162	/**
   163	 * The four numbers, or an honest account of why there are not four numbers.
   164	 *
   165	 * A failed read and a student who has never run this section produce the same
   166	 * empty array and must not produce the same sentence: one of them is being told
   167	 * her work is gone.
   168	 */
   169	function StatStrip({
   170	  state,
   171	  stats,
   172	}: {
   173	  state: HistoryState;
   174	  stats: ReturnType<typeof statsFor>;
   175	}) {
   176	  if (state.kind === "loading") {
   177	    return (
   178	      <div className="sl-stats" aria-hidden="true">
   179	        {[0, 1, 2, 3].map((i) => (
   180	          <div className="sl-stat" key={i}>
   181	            <span className="sl-label">&nbsp;</span>
   182	            <span className="sl-sv">
   183	              <span className="sl-skel" style={{ width: "60%" }} />
   184	            </span>
   185	          </div>
   186	        ))}
   187	      </div>
   188	    );
   189	  }
   190	  if (state.kind === "failed" || state.kind === "idle") return null;
   191	  if (stats.totalRuns === 0) return null;
   192	
   193	  return (
   194	    <div className="sl-stats">
   195	      <div className="sl-stat">

   197	        <span className="sl-sv" data-empty={stats.lastRunSeconds === null || undefined}>
   198	          {stats.lastRunSeconds === null ? "no runs yet" : clock(stats.lastRunSeconds)}
   199	        </span>

   201	      </div>
   202	      <div className="sl-stat">
   203	        <span className="sl-label">Trend</span>
   204	        <span

   206	          data-dir={(stats.trendSeconds ?? 0) < 0 ? "down" : undefined}
   207	          data-empty={stats.trendSeconds === null || undefined}
   208	        >
   209	          {stats.trendSeconds === null ? "first run" : signedClock(stats.trendSeconds)}
   210	        </span>
   211	        <span className="sl-sd">
   212	          {stats.trendSeconds === null ? "nothing to compare yet" : `across ${stats.trendRuns} runs`}
   213	        </span>
   214	      </div>
   215	      <div className="sl-stat">
   216	        <span className="sl-label">Recalled</span>
   217	        <span className="sl-sv" data-empty={stats.recalled === null || undefined}>

   219	          {stats.recalledOf !== null && <small>/{stats.recalledOf}</small>}
   220	        </span>
   221	        <span className="sl-sd">
   222	          {stats.recalled === null ? "not recorded" : "stimulus by stimulus"}
   223	        </span>
   224	      </div>
   225	      <div className="sl-stat">
   226	        <span className="sl-label">Target</span>

   229	        </span>
   230	        <span className="sl-sd">
   231	          {stats.targetMinutes === null
   232	            ? "no clock set"
   233	            : stats.targetRuns === 0
   234	              ? "no run on this clock yet"
   235	              : `met on ${stats.metTarget} of ${stats.targetRuns}`}
   236	        </span>
   237	      </div>
   238	    </div>
   239	  );
   240	}
   241	
   242	/** Her last few runs, and what each one covered. */
   243	function RunHistory({ state }: { state: HistoryState }) {

   245	
   246	  return (

     1	import type { ReactNode } from "react";
     2	import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
     3	import { SectionLaunchFrame } from "./SectionLaunchFrame";
     4	import "./section-launch.css";
     5	
     6	export interface SectionLibraryLaunchProps {
     7	  title: string;
     8	  subtitle: string | null;
     9	  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
    10	  /** The list itself, which owns the sections and the starting. */
    11	  children: ReactNode;
    12	  onExit?: () => void;
    13	}
    14	
    15	/**
    16	 * The door to Read, Recall, Colour: the method, and the sections it is run on.
    17	 *
    18	 * One drill, and every section reachable from the page that explains it. Before
    19	 * this there were three screens between her and a run — an editorial intro
    20	 * written for the typed Activities, a bare list, and then the section's own
    21	 * page — and only the last of them was designed for this drill.
    22	 *
    23	 * It carries no stat strip, and that is a refusal rather than an omission.
    24	 * Trend and Target are per section: sections differ in length and in the clock
    25	 * they are run against, so "−6:52 across 3 runs" spanning two different
    26	 * sections would be a number about nothing. Each section row carries its own
    27	 * attempts instead, and the four numbers appear on the section's own page,
    28	 * where they mean something.
    29	 */
    30	export function SectionLibraryLaunch({
    31	  title,
    32	  subtitle,
    33	  instructions,
    34	  children,
    35	  onExit,
    36	}: SectionLibraryLaunchProps) {
    37	  return (
    38	    <SectionLaunchFrame
    39	      title={title}
    40	      subtitle={subtitle}
    41	      instructions={instructions}
    42	      onExit={onExit}
    43	      showBreadcrumbs={false}
    44	      sectionsFirst
    45	    >
    46	      <div className="sl-runs">
    47	        <div>
    48	          <span className="sl-label">Your sections</span>
    49	          <div style={{ marginTop: 12 }}>{children}</div>
    50	        </div>
    51	
    52	        <aside className="sl-aside">
    53	          <span className="sl-label">Before you start</span>
    54	          <p>
    55	            Read each piece silently. Then look away and say it back in your own
    56	            words. Give your reaction out loud too. After the final piece,
    57	            recall the whole argument out loud from memory. Your entire section
    58	            is recorded in <strong>one take</strong>, and you cannot pause the
    59	            recording.
    60	          </p>
    61	          <p>
    62	            Every completed attempt stays here. If you run a section again, it
    63	            is saved as a new attempt. Your earlier attempts are never replaced.
    64	          </p>
    65	        </aside>
    66	      </div>
    67	    </SectionLaunchFrame>
    68	  );
    69	}

     1	import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useState } from "react";
     2	import {
     3	  listMySectionGrades,
     4	  listSectionChoices,
     5	  startSectionAttempt,
     6	  type SectionChoice,
     7	} from "../api/activitiesClient";
     8	import type { ActivityAssignmentSummary, SectionGradeStub } from "../api/types";
     9	
    10	/**
    11	 * The sections she can run, and what she has already done.
    12	 *
    13	 * Read, Recall, Colour is one Activity now. Joshua assigns the drill; she picks
    14	 * which section to work tonight, and she works several. That is why this list
    15	 * exists at all: a section per library card meant he chose for her, and it grew
    16	 * by one card every time a section was built.
    17	 *
    18	 * Two rules the drill puts on this screen:
    19	 *
    20	 * - **Nothing is hidden once done.** The regular section picker drops a section
    21	 *   she has sat, which is right when there are two hundred of them and wrong
    22	 *   here, where there are three. Repeating a section is legitimate: she is
    23	 *   training recall, not measuring a score.
    24	 * - **Every Attempt is kept.** Not a count, not the latest. Attempt 1, 2 and 3
    25	 *   each keep their own date and their own Report Card, because the point of
    26	 *   running a section twice is seeing what changed.
    27	 *
    28	 * Starting is a server call, not an insert. She chooses the section; the row
    29	 * that holds her run is built under definer rights, because the snapshot
    30	 * carries the Details her Coverage is scored against and must never be hers to
    31	 * write.
    32	 */
    33	export interface SectionLibraryProps {
    34	  /** Her assignments, used to show what she has already attempted. */
    35	  attempts: ActivityAssignmentSummary[];
    36	  /** Called with the new assignment id once an Attempt has been created. */
    37	  onStart: (assignmentId: string) => void;
    38	  /**
    39	   * Called with the assignment of an Attempt she wants to look at again.
    40	   *
    41	   * Its own Report Card is what opens: a completed section routes there rather
    42	   * than to the generic summary, from any device and at any later date.
    43	   */
    44	  onOpenAttempt?: (assignmentId: string) => void;
    45	  /** Injected in tests. */
    46	  loadChoices?: () => Promise<SectionChoice[]>;
    47	  loadGrades?: () => Promise<SectionGradeStub[]>;
    48	  beginAttempt?: (activityId: string) => Promise<string>;
    49	}
    50	
    51	function attemptDate(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string {
    52	  const when = new Date(a.completed_at ?? a.created_at);
    53	  return when.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: "numeric", month: "short" });
    54	}
    55	
    56	/**
    57	 * A section she was handed and has not opened.
    58	 *
    59	 * Not an Attempt: nothing was recorded, nothing was answered, and calling it
    60	 * "Attempt 1 - not finished" tells her she abandoned a run she never began.
    61	 * `started_at` is the honest test, because a teacher-assigned row and a
    62	 * server-made one both arrive as rows and only one of them has a clock.
    63	 */
    64	function isWaiting(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): boolean {
    65	  return a.status === "assigned" && !a.started_at;
    66	}
    67	
    68	/**

    73	 * The old list said "done" for all three and led nowhere, so an Attempt she had
    74	 * spent twenty-five minutes on was a line of grey text.

    76	function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {

    79	  if (!grade) return `${date} · still being marked`;
    80	  return `${date} · ${grade.section.mean_coverage}% carried`;

   101	  if (!grade) return "Check back shortly";
   102	  return "See what you missed";

   105	export function SectionLibrary({
   106	  attempts,
   107	  onStart,
   108	  onOpenAttempt,
   109	  loadChoices = listSectionChoices,
   110	  loadGrades = listMySectionGrades,
   111	  beginAttempt = startSectionAttempt,
   112	}: SectionLibraryProps) {
   113	  const [choices, setChoices] = useState<SectionChoice[] | null>(null);
   114	  /**
   115	   * Her Report Cards, by assignment.
   116	   *
   117	   * Loaded beside the sections and never waited on: the list is useful without
   118	   * them, and a failed read costs a number on a row rather than the screen.
   119	   */
   120	  const [grades, setGrades] = useState<Map<string, SectionGradeStub>>(new Map());

   126	    void (async () => {
   127	      try {
   128	        const rows = await loadChoices();

   130	      } catch {
   131	        // Named plainly. An empty list and a failed read are different facts,
   132	        // and only one of them means "nothing to do tonight".
   133	        if (!cancelled) setError("Could not load the sections. Try again in a moment.");
   134	      }
   135	    })();
   136	    return () => {
   137	      cancelled = true;
   138	    };
   139	  }, [loadChoices]);
   140	
   141	  useEffect(() => {
   142	    let cancelled = false;

   144	      try {
   145	        const rows = await loadGrades();
   146	        if (cancelled) return;
   147	        // Newest Attempt per assignment. `listMySectionGrades` returns them
   148	        // newest first, so the first one seen for an assignment wins.
   149	        const byAssignment = new Map<string, SectionGradeStub>();
   150	        for (const g of rows) if (!byAssignment.has(g.assignment_id)) byAssignment.set(g.assignment_id, g);
   151	        setGrades(byAssignment);

   154	      }
   155	    })();

   158	    };

   161	  /** Her attempts, newest first, grouped by which section they were on. */
   162	  const bySection = useMemo(() => {
   163	    const map = new Map<string, ActivityAssignmentSummary[]>();
   164	    for (const a of attempts) {
   165	      if (a.mode !== "section" || !a.source_section) continue;
   166	      // A row she has never opened is not a run. See `waitingBySection`.
   167	      if (isWaiting(a)) continue;
   168	      const list = map.get(a.source_section) ?? [];
   169	      list.push(a);
   170	      map.set(a.source_section, list);
   171	    }
   172	    for (const list of map.values()) {
   173	      list.sort((x, y) => new Date(y.created_at).getTime() - new Date(x.created_at).getTime());
   174	    }
   175	    return map;

   177	
   178	  /**
   179	   * Sections Joshua handed her that she has not opened yet.

   181	   * These carry their own message and their own classroom link, so the row IS

   183	   */
   184	  const waitingBySection = useMemo(() => {
   185	    const map = new Map<string, ActivityAssignmentSummary>();

   188	      const held = map.get(a.source_section);
   189	      // Oldest wins: it is the one whose message she was actually sent.
   190	      if (!held || new Date(a.created_at) < new Date(held.created_at)) {

   197	  const start = useCallback(

   224	  );

   226	  if (error && !choices) {

   228	  }
   229	  if (!choices) {
   230	    return <p className="sl-note">Loading sections\u2026</p>;
   231	  }
   232	  /*
   233	   * An empty list is a fact, not a blank space.
   234	   *
   235	   * "Choose a section" carries her to this slab, and a slab with nothing in it
   236	   * is the dead end she reports as the drill being broken. Say what is true:
   237	   * nothing has been built for her yet.
   238	   */
   239	  if (choices.length === 0) {
   240	    return (
   241	      <p className="sl-note">
   242	        No sections are ready for you yet. Joshua adds them here as they are built.
   243	        Check back, or message him if you were expecting one tonight.
   244	      </p>
   245	    );
   246	  }

   249	    <div className="sl-sections">
   250	      {error && (

   254	      )}
   255	      {choices.map((choice) => {

   258	          <div key={choice.id} className="sl-section" data-section={choice.source_section}>
   259	            <div className="sl-sec-body">
   260	              <div className="sl-sec-line">
   261	                <span className="sl-sec-name">{choice.source_section}</span>
   262	                {/*
   263	                  * The time, not the stimulus count.
   264	                  *
   265	                  * A stimulus is our grain, not hers, and the counts differ per
   266	                  * section for a reason she has no way to see: two questions can
   267	                  * share a stimulus. Printed on a row to choose from, "22" next
   268	                  * to "26" reads as one section being longer, which is not what
   269	                  * it means. The clock is the thing she actually plans around.
   270	                  */}
   271	                <span className="sl-sec-clock">
   272	                  {choice.goal_minutes?.standard ?? 25} min
   273	                </span>
   274	              </div>
   275	
   276	              {done.length === 0 ? (
   277	                <p className="sl-note">Not run yet.</p>
   278	              ) : (
   279	                /*
   280	                 * Every Attempt, and every one of them a way back in.
   281	                 *
   282	                 * The Report Card used to exist only in the tab that produced it:
   283	                 * this list rendered the same runs as plain text, so a grade she
   284	                 * could not have seen -- grading finishes minutes after she stops
   285	                 * -- was unreachable for ever after. A finished Attempt opens on
   286	                 * its own Report Card; an unfinished one is not a run and does
   287	                 * not pretend to lead anywhere.
   288	                 */
   289	                <ul className="sl-sec-attempts">
   290	                  {done.map((a, i) => {
   291	                    const grade = grades.get(a.id);
   292	                    const label = `Attempt ${done.length - i} \u00b7 ${attemptLabel(a, grade)}`;
   293	                    const action = attemptAction(a, grade);
   294	                    const reachable = a.status === "completed" && onOpenAttempt !== undefined;
   295	                    return (
   296	                      <li key={a.id}>
   297	                        {reachable ? (
   298	                          <button
   299	                            type="button"
   300	                            className="sl-sec-attempt"
   301	                            onClick={() => onOpenAttempt?.(a.id)}
   302	                          >
   303	                            <span className="sl-sec-attempt-label">{label}</span>
   304	                            {action && <span className="sl-sec-attempt-go">{action}</span>}
   305	                          </button>
   306	                        ) : (
   307	                          <span className="sl-sec-attempt" data-flat="true">
   308	                            <span className="sl-sec-attempt-label">{label}</span>
   309	                            {action && <span className="sl-sec-attempt-go">{action}</span>}
   310	                          </span>
   311	                        )}
   312	                      </li>
   313	                    );
   314	                  })}
   315	                </ul>
   316	              )}
   317	            </div>
   318	
   319	            <button
   320	              type="button"

   326	            </button>
   327	          </div>
   328	        );
   329	      })}
   330	    </div>
   331	  );
   332	}

     1	import { useEffect, useState } from "react";
     2	import { listMySectionGrades } from "../api/activitiesClient";
     3	import type { SectionGradeStub } from "../api/types";
     4	
     5	/**
     6	 * Every graded Read, Recall, Colour run this student has, oldest to newest.
     7	 *
     8	 * Each finished section is already stored whole -- its coverage, its per-line
     9	 * marking, its transcript and the recording behind it -- but until now the only
    10	 * way to see one was to remember which Attempt it was and open it. A student
    11	 * who runs a section a week has no way of knowing whether the weeks are adding
    12	 * up to anything, and that is the entire point of grading her.
    13	 *
    14	 * So: one row per graded run, in date order, with the number that moves. The
    15	 * bars are the same number drawn, because a column of percentages is a list and
    16	 * a shape is a trend.
    17	 */
    18	export function SectionProgress({
    19	  loadGrades = listMySectionGrades,
    20	  onOpen,
    21	}: {
    22	  loadGrades?: () => Promise<SectionGradeStub[]>;
    23	  onOpen?: (assignmentId: string) => void;
    24	}) {
    25	  const [runs, setRuns] = useState<SectionGradeStub[] | null>(null);
    26	  const [failed, setFailed] = useState(false);
    27	
    28	  useEffect(() => {
    29	    let cancelled = false;
    30	    void (async () => {
    31	      try {
    32	        const rows = await loadGrades();
    33	        if (!cancelled) setRuns(rows);
    34	      } catch {
    35	        if (!cancelled) setFailed(true);
    36	      }
    37	    })();
    38	    return () => {
    39	      cancelled = true;
    40	    };
    41	  }, [loadGrades]);
    42	
    43	  if (failed) {
    44	    return (
    45	      <section className="rounded-2xl border border-border/60 bg-card/40 p-6">
    46	        <h2 className="text-sm font-semibold tracking-wide text-foreground">Your progress</h2>
    47	        <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
    48	          Your past runs could not be loaded just now. They are saved — try again in a moment.
    49	        </p>
    50	      </section>
    51	    );
    52	  }
    53	
    54	  if (runs === null) {
    55	    return (
    56	      <section className="rounded-2xl border border-border/60 bg-card/40 p-6">
    57	        <h2 className="text-sm font-semibold tracking-wide text-foreground">Your progress</h2>
    58	        <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-muted-foreground">Loading your past runs…</p>
    59	      </section>
    60	    );
    61	  }
    62	
    63	  if (runs.length === 0) {
    64	    return (
    65	      <section className="rounded-2xl border border-border/60 bg-card/40 p-6">
    66	        <h2 className="text-sm font-semibold tracking-wide text-foreground">Your progress</h2>
    67	        <p className="mt-2 max-w-[60ch] text-sm text-muted-foreground">
    68	          No graded runs yet. Finish a section below and its marked report is saved here — every
    69	          run you do from then on shows up in this list.
    70	        </p>
    71	      </section>
    72	    );
    73	  }
    74	
    75	  // Oldest first: the eye reads left to right, and improvement should run that
    76	  // way too. The query hands them back newest first.
    77	  const ordered = [...runs].reverse();
    78	  const latest = ordered[ordered.length - 1];
    79	  const first = ordered[0];
    80	  const change = Math.round(pct(latest) - pct(first));
    81	
    82	  return (
    83	    <section className="rounded-2xl border border-border/60 bg-card/40 p-6">
    84	      <div className="flex flex-wrap items-baseline justify-between gap-3">
    85	        <h2 className="text-sm font-semibold tracking-wide text-foreground">Your progress</h2>
    86	        <p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">
    87	          {ordered.length} graded {ordered.length === 1 ? "run" : "runs"}
    88	          {ordered.length > 1 && (
    89	            <>
    90	              {" · "}
    91	              {change > 0 ? `up ${change}` : change < 0 ? `down ${Math.abs(change)}` : "level"}
    92	              {change === 0 ? "" : " points"} since your first
    93	            </>
    94	          )}
    95	        </p>
    96	      </div>
    97	
    98	      <div className="mt-5 flex h-24 items-end gap-1.5" aria-hidden="true">
    99	        {ordered.map((r) => (
   100	          <div
   101	            key={`${r.assignment_id}-${r.attempt_no}`}
   102	            className="min-w-[6px] flex-1 rounded-t bg-primary/70"
   103	            style={{ height: `${Math.max(4, pct(r))}%` }}
   104	          />
   105	        ))}
   106	      </div>
   107	
   108	      <ul className="mt-5 divide-y divide-border/50">
   109	        {[...ordered].reverse().map((r) => (
   110	          <li key={`${r.assignment_id}-${r.attempt_no}`}>
   111	            <button
   112	              type="button"
   113	              onClick={() => onOpen?.(r.assignment_id)}
   114	              disabled={!onOpen}
   115	              className="flex w-full items-center justify-between gap-4 py-3 text-left disabled:cursor-default"
   116	            >
   117	              <span className="min-w-0">
   118	                <span className="block truncate text-sm text-foreground">
   119	                  {r.source_section || "Section"}
   120	                  {r.attempt_no > 1 ? ` · attempt ${r.attempt_no}` : ""}
   121	                </span>
   122	                <span className="block text-xs text-muted-foreground">
   123	                  {formatWhen(r.graded_at)}
   124	                  {r.section?.total_seconds
   125	                    ? ` · ${Math.round(r.section.total_seconds / 60)} min`
   126	                    : ""}
   127	                  {r.section?.reciting_count ? ` · ${r.section.reciting_count} recited` : ""}
   128	                </span>
   129	              </span>
   130	              <span className="shrink-0 text-sm font-semibold tabular-nums text-foreground">
   131	                {Math.round(pct(r))}%
   132	              </span>
   133	            </button>
   134	          </li>
   135	        ))}
   136	      </ul>
   137	    </section>
   138	  );
   139	}
   140	
   141	/** Coverage is stored 0-1 in some runs and 0-100 in others; read both. */
   142	function pct(r: SectionGradeStub): number {
   143	  const v = r.section?.mean_coverage ?? 0;
   144	  const n = v <= 1 ? v * 100 : v;
   145	  return Math.max(0, Math.min(100, n));
   146	}
   147	
   148	function formatWhen(iso: string): string {
   149	  const d = new Date(iso);
   150	  if (Number.isNaN(d.getTime())) return "";
   151	  return d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { month: "short", day: "numeric", year: "numeric" });
   152	}

     1	import { useMemo } from "react";
     2	import { motion, useReducedMotion, useTransform, type MotionValue } from "framer-motion";
     3	
     4	/**
     5	 * The dusk meadow behind the Read, Recall, Colour door.
     6	 *
     7	 * Painted, not photographed, and painted at render time: every ridge is a
     8	 * seeded sum of sines, every blade of grass a seeded quadratic, so the scene
     9	 * ships as geometry rather than as three PNGs that have to arrive. It is one
    10	 * picture cut into five depth planes -- sky, far hills, mid hills, near hills,
    11	 * foreground grass -- and each plane is its own layer so the door can move them
    12	 * at different rates as she scrolls. The demo card sits BETWEEN the near hills
    13	 * and the grass, which is what makes the grass pass in front of it.
    14	 *
    15	 * Palette is the runner's own indigo field lifted into a sky, so the door and
    16	 * the drill room read as one evening.
    17	 */
    18	
    19	const W = 1600;
    20	const H = 900;
    21	
    22	function mulberry32(seed: number) {
    23	  let t = seed;
    24	  return () => {
    25	    t += 0x6d2b79f5;
    26	    let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
    27	    r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
    28	    return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
    29	  };
    30	}
    31	
    32	/** A smooth ridge: a few sines summed, closed to the bottom edge. */
    33	function ridge(seed: number, base: number, amp: number): string {
    34	  const rng = mulberry32(seed);
    35	  const waves = Array.from({ length: 4 }, (_, i) => ({
    36	    f: (0.6 + rng() * 1.4) * (i + 1) * 0.0018,
    37	    p: rng() * Math.PI * 2,
    38	    a: amp / (i + 1.2),
    39	  }));
    40	  const pts: string[] = [];
    41	  for (let x = -40; x <= W + 40; x += 16) {
    42	    const y = waves.reduce((s, w) => s + w.a * Math.sin(w.f * x + w.p), base);
    43	    pts.push(`${x.toFixed(1)} ${y.toFixed(1)}`);
    44	  }
    45	  // Closed 700 units below the frame, not at its edge: the plane rises as she
    46	  // scrolls, and the skirt is what keeps the ground beneath it painted.
    47	  return `M -40 ${H + 700} L ${pts.join(" L ")} L ${W + 40} ${H + 700} Z`;
    48	}
    49	
    50	interface Blade {
    51	  d: string;
    52	  w: number;
    53	  c: string;
    54	  delay: number;
    55	  x: number;
    56	}
    57	interface Flower {
    58	  x: number;
    59	  y: number;
    60	  r: number;
    61	  c: string;
    62	}
    63	
    64	const GREENS = ["#4f6d55", "#5f7d64", "#6f8f74", "#7d9a80", "#8fa896"];
    65	const FLOWERS = ["#f2b544", "#f7d27a", "#f6f3ea", "#c9c3ec", "#f3c9a0"];
    66	
    67	function grass(seed: number, count: number): { blades: Blade[]; flowers: Flower[] } {
    68	  const rng = mulberry32(seed);
    69	  const blades: Blade[] = [];
    70	  const flowers: Flower[] = [];
    71	  for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
    72	    const x = -20 + rng() * (W + 40);
    73	    const h = 36 + rng() * 96;
    74	    const lean = (rng() - 0.5) * 60;
    75	    const y0 = H + 10;
    76	    blades.push({
    77	      x,
    78	      d: `M ${x.toFixed(1)} ${y0} Q ${(x + lean * 0.35).toFixed(1)} ${(y0 - h * 0.62).toFixed(1)} ${(x + lean).toFixed(1)} ${(y0 - h).toFixed(1)}`,
    79	      w: 2.5 + rng() * 4,
    80	      c: GREENS[Math.floor(rng() * GREENS.length)],
    81	      delay: rng() * 4,
    82	    });
    83	    if (rng() < 0.22) {
    84	      flowers.push({
    85	        x: x + lean,
    86	        y: y0 - h - 3,
    87	        r: 3 + rng() * 4.5,
    88	        c: FLOWERS[Math.floor(rng() * FLOWERS.length)],
    89	      });
    90	    }
    91	  }
    92	  return { blades, flowers };
    93	}

    95	export interface MeadowSceneProps {
    96	  /** 0 at the top of the hero, 1 when the hero has scrolled fully past. */
    97	  progress: MotionValue<number>;
    98	  /** Content drawn between the near hills and the foreground grass. */

   100	}

   109	export function MeadowScene({ progress, children }: MeadowSceneProps) {
   110	  const reduced = useReducedMotion();
   111	
   112	  const far = useMemo(() => ridge(11, 545, 42), []);

   118	  // Each plane rides the scroll at its own rate. Negative is "up and away":

   127

   129

   146	            <stop offset="1" stopColor="#f3c9a0" stopOpacity="0" />
   147	          </radialGradient>
   148	        </defs>
   149	        <rect width={W} height={H} fill="url(#mw-sky-g)" />
   150	        <rect width={W} height={H} fill="url(#mw-sun)" />
